<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every positive integer <i>n</i> other than 2, 4, and 9, we prove that some irreducible complex representation of <i>S</i><sub><i>n</i></sub> has a tensor square containing every irreducible representation. This resolves the tensor square conjecture for symmetric groups affirmatively.

- **claim-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every positive integer $n\notin\{2,4,9\}$ there is a partition $\lambda\vdash n$ such that $$g(\lambda,\lambda,\nu)>0\qquad\text{for every }\nu\vdash n.$$ Thus one irreducible representation has a tensor square containing every irreducible representation of $S_n$.
  - Explanation: For every degree outside three exceptional ones, one fixed self-conjugate partition has a Kronecker square containing every irreducible representation of the symmetric group.
- **claim-unipotent-squares** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-unipotent-squares)): For each positive integer $n\notin\{2,4,9\}$, choose a partition $\lambda_n$ supplied by Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, including its finite-range witnesses. For every prime power $q$, let $U_q^\lambda$ denote the unipotent irreducible complex character of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n(\mathbb F_q)$ indexed by $\lambda\vdash n$. Then $U_q^{\lambda_n}\otimes U_q^{\lambda_n}$ contains $U_q^\nu$ for every $\nu\vdash n$.
  - Explanation: The covering property transfers to unipotent characters of finite general linear groups for the same partition labels and every prime power.
- **claim-dual-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dual-test)): Let $z\in E^{\otimes B}$ and $\theta\vdash \|B\|$ with at most $D$ rows. Then $S^\theta$ occurs in $\mathbb C[S_B]z$ if and only if $z$ has a nonzero contraction with a dual block tensor whose blocks are the columns of $\theta$, for some placement of the blocks and some common ordered basis of $E^*$. If such a contraction exists for a fixed placement, it is nonzero for a generic ordered basis. Finitely many separately nonzero contractions can be made nonzero simultaneously with a common generic basis.
  - Explanation: A Specht module occurs in a cyclic tensor module exactly when a contraction with a dual column-alternating tensor is nonzero; genericity of the basis makes finitely many contractions nonzero together.
- **claim-young-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-young-support)): For a finite list $A$ of positive integers of sum $n$, the module $\mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}_{\prod_{a\in A}S_a}^{S_n}\mathbf1$ contains exactly the $S^\nu$ whose column heights $h_1\ge h_2\ge\cdots$ satisfy $$\label{eq:young-prefix} \sum_{j=1}^k h_j\le P_A(k):=\sum_{a\in A}\min(k,a) \qquad(k\ge1).$$ The constituent indexed by the decreasing rearrangement of $A$ has multiplicity one. A component containing every shape of total $t$ with at most $j$ rows contains the support of the induced-trivial module corresponding to a balanced division of $t$ into $j$ parts.
  - Explanation: Constituents of an induced trivial module are described by upper bounds on prefix sums of column heights, which also yields balanced-division supports.
- **claim-lr-addition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lr-addition)): If $c_{\alpha,\beta}^{\gamma}>0$ and $c_{\alpha',\beta'}^{\gamma'}>0$, then $$c_{\alpha+\alpha',\,\beta+\beta'}^{\gamma+\gamma'}>0,$$ where addition is rowwise. In particular, suppose a target has column heights $h_i=a_i+b_i$, with $a_i,b_i\ge0$. Form $\alpha$ from the column-height list $(a_i)$ and $\beta$ from $(b_i)$, omitting zeros and sorting each list. Then $c_{\alpha,\beta}^{\gamma}>0$ for the target $\gamma$.
  - Explanation: Positivity of Littlewood-Richardson coefficients is preserved under rowwise addition, so column-height splittings of a target give positive coefficients.
- **claim-cyclic-input** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-cyclic-input)): For every $m\ge1$ and every $\alpha\vdash N_m$, the irreducible $S^\alpha$ occurs in the particular cyclic module $W_m$ of <a href="#eq:cyclic-generator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cyclic-generator">[eq:cyclic-generator]</a>.
  - Explanation: Imported result: the specified row-column cyclic module on a staircase contains every irreducible of the triangular degree.
- **claim-candidate** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-candidate)): The parameters satisfy $$\label{eq:r-bounds} r\le\begin{cases}(M-1)/2,&M\text{ odd},\\3M/2+2,&M\text{ even}. \end{cases}$$ For $M\ge9$, the partition with column lengths $$\label{eq:candidate} L=(M+b+s-1,\ M-1+b,\ M-2,M-3,\ldots,3,\ 2^{b+1},\ 1^s)$$ is self-conjugate and has size $n$. Consequently the support of $S^L\otimes S^L$ is invariant under transposition of the target. If no parity-compatible $M\ge9$ is available, then $n\le64$.
  - Explanation: The parameter bounds hold and the explicit column list defines a self-conjugate partition of the right size, so its square is symmetric under transposing targets; small degrees are the only ones left over.
- **claim-band-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-band-test)): Let $M\ge9$, and let $\lambda\vdash \|L\|$ have column heights $h_1\ge h_2\ge\cdots$ and row lengths $w_1\ge w_2\ge\cdots$, with zeros appended. Suppose that integers $d,q$ satisfy $1\le d\le4$, $0\le q\le8$, and $$\label{eq:band-criterion} \sum_{i=1}^d w_i\ge K,\qquad dq+7+d\le K,\qquad \sum_{i=1}^q \max\left(0,\left\lfloor\frac{h_i-d}{2}\right\rfloor\right) \ge r.$$ Then $S^\lambda$ occurs in $S^L\otimes S^L$.
  - Explanation: Three numerical inequalities on row sums, endpoint room and even column capacities guarantee that a target occurs in the candidate square.
- **claim-sector-induction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sector-induction)): Let a finite group $G$ act transitively on a finite set $\Omega$. Suppose a $G$-module has a direct-sum decomposition $T=\bigoplus_{A\in\Omega}T_A$ with $gT_A=T_{gA}$. Fix $D\in\Omega$, let $H$ be its stabilizer, and let $z\in T_D$. Writing $A_0=\mathbb C[H]z$, the map $$\mathbb C[G]\otimes_{\mathbb C[H]}A_0\longrightarrow\mathbb C[G]z, \qquad g\otimes a\longmapsto ga,$$ is an isomorphism. In particular, the cyclic module on the right is the full induced module $\mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}_H^G A_0$.
  - Explanation: A cyclic module generated inside one sector of a transitively permuted decomposition is the full induced module from the stabilizer.
- **claim-triangle-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-band-triangle-quotient)): There is a quotient of $W_L$ isomorphic to $$\label{eq:band-triangle-quotient} \mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}_{S_D\times S_F}^{S_B} (W_{M-2}\boxtimes U_F),$$ where $W_{M-2}$ is the specified cyclic module in Theorem <a href="#thm:cyclic-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:cyclic-input">6</a>. The module $U_F$ is generated by the row and column block alternations on $F$, using an initial alphabet on every remaining block. Consequently, it suffices to find a diagram $\mu\subseteq\lambda$ of size $\|F\|$ such that $S^\mu$ occurs in $U_F$.
  - Explanation: The cyclic module of the enlarged diagram has an induced quotient from the inner staircase module and a boundary module, reducing the task to the boundary.
- **claim-band-separated** (theorem, [source](#stmt-band-separated)): Retain from $u_F$ only terms using $P$ on the path, $X_+$ on $E_+$, and $X_-$ on $E_-$. The resulting tensor is, up to one nonzero overall sign, $$\label{eq:band-separated} u_{\mathrm{path}}\otimes z_+\otimes z_-.$$ Here $u_{\mathrm{path}}$ is the product of the two path block alternations, with both endpoint singleton letters equal to one. The tensors $z_+,z_-$ are the row-column alternating generators for the two copies of $E$, with the long-block index shifted by two and the two tensor layers interchanged for $E_-$.
  - Explanation: Restricting letters splits the boundary generator into a path alternation and two attachment generators, up to sign.
- **claim-path-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-band-path-support)): Let $k=2t+1$ with $t\ge0$. On positions $1,\ldots,k$, put alternating pairs in the first layer on $(2,3),(4,5),\ldots,(k-1,k)$ and the singleton letter $1$ at position $1$. In the second layer put alternating pairs on $(1,2),(3,4),\ldots,(k-2,k-1)$ and the singleton letter $1$ at position $k$. Denote the resulting pair-letter tensor by $u$.* *For every ordered list $a_1,\ldots,a_c$ with $1\le a_i\le4$ and $\sum_i a_i=k$, place the columns of a dual polytabloid consecutively along the path with these lengths. There is a common ordered basis of $\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}_2(\mathbb C)$ for which its contraction with $u$ is nonzero. In particular, the cyclic span of $u$ contains every partition of $k$ with at most four rows. For any finite collection of such paths and ordered column lists, one common generic basis makes all their contractions nonzero.
  - Explanation: A path of alternating pairs contracts nontrivially with consecutive columns of height at most four via words of two-by-two matrices, so every partition with at most four rows occurs.
- **claim-one-extra** (theorem, [source](#stmt-band-one-extra)): Let $E=(b+\delta,b)$, where $b\ge0$ and $\delta\in\{0,1\}$, and let $Z_E$ be the cyclic span of its row-column alternating generator. The sign twist $Z_E\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}$ contains every partition $\alpha\vdash 2b+\delta$ with at most four rows such that all its row lengths are even when $\delta=0$, or exactly one row length is odd when $\delta=1$.
  - Explanation: A single two-row attachment, after a sign twist, supports four-row partitions with the stated parity of row lengths.
- **claim-two-extras** (theorem, [source](#stmt-band-two-extras)): The cyclic span of $z_+\otimes z_-$ on $E_+\sqcup E_-$ contains every diagram $\eta\vdash 2r$ whose column heights are even and whose number of columns is at most eight.
  - Explanation: The two attachments together support every diagram with even column heights and a bounded number of columns.
- **claim-balance-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-balance-separation)): Partition the column blocks of $L$ into collections $B_1,\ldots,B_v$ on disjoint position sets $D_1,\ldots,D_v$, using the same blocks in both factors. Suppose $I_1,\ldots,I_v$ are nonempty sets of integers with every element of $I_i$ smaller than every element of $I_{i+1}$. Let $W_i$ be the image of $Y_{B_i}\otimes Y_{B_i}$ under the coordinate projection retaining words all of whose outputs lie in $I_i$. Then the support of $S^L\otimes S^L$ contains the support of $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}_{S_{d_{B_1}}\times\cdots\times S_{d_{B_v}}}^{S_n} (W_1\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes W_v).$$
  - Explanation: Splitting column blocks over disjoint output intervals lets the global projection factor, so the induced module of the local projected components lies inside the square.
- **claim-coordinate-line** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bal-coordinate-line)): Let $X=S^\mu$ be realized by column-alternating vectors in the word space of content $\mu=(\mu_1,\mu_2,\ldots)$. For a word $a$ of this content, let $R_a$ permute separately the positions carrying each equal letter. The restriction $\epsilon_a\|_X$ of its coordinate functional is nonzero. Under $X^*\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\simeq S^{\mu^t}$, its line is the line of the column-alternating vector on the equal-letter blocks of $a$, whose lengths are $\mu_1,\mu_2,\ldots$.
  - Explanation: A surviving word coordinate restricted to a column-alternating realization gives, after the sign twist, the alternating vector on the equal-letter blocks.
- **claim-neighboring** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bal-neighboring)): For every integer $u\ge1$, the local component with column lengths $u,u-1$, projected to outputs in $[u-1,u]$, contains every type in $\mathcal H_4(2u-1)$.
  - Explanation: Neighboring column lengths, projected to a narrow output window, give an odd path whose support is every type with at most four rows.
- **claim-two-paths** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bal-two-paths)): For every integer $q\ge0$, the component with column lengths $q+2,q$, projected to outputs in $[q,2q+3]$, contains $\mathcal O_4(2q+2)$. If $q\ge3$, inducing this support together with a trivial representation of size two contains every member of $\mathcal H_4(2q+4)$.
  - Explanation: The component with two long columns supports types with an odd-height column, and inducing with a small trivial module extends this to all types with at most four rows.
- **claim-even-rows** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bal-even-rows)): Let $m\ge0$ be an integer, let $E$ have dimension $j$, and let $H\in\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2 E$ be nondegenerate. The position-permutation span of $H^{\otimes m}\in E^{\otimes 2m}$ contains every member of $\mathcal E_j(2m)$. Moreover, the polynomial $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(E)$-module $\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^m(\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2 E)$ contains the Schur module of each such partition.
  - Explanation: Repeated nondegenerate symmetric pairs support every partition with even row lengths and few rows, matching Littlewood's decomposition.
- **claim-odd-strip** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bal-odd-strip)): Let $c$ be positive and even, and let $x\in\{1,3\}$ with $x\le c$. Inducing the support $\mathcal E_2(c)$ with the trivial representation of size $x$ supplies $\mathcal H_2(c+x)$.
  - Explanation: Pieri induction of the even-row two-row support with a small odd trivial module covers all two-row partitions of the combined size.
- **claim-balance-four** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-balance-four)): Assume $M\ge9$, and let $h_1\ge h_2\ge\cdots$ be the column heights of $\nu\vdash n$. If $$\sum_{i=1}^4 h_i\le2M-2, \label{bal:four-small}$$ then $S^\nu$ occurs in $S^L\otimes S^L$. The same conclusion holds if Equation <a href="#bal:four-small" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bal:four-small">[bal:four-small]</a> holds for the column heights of $\nu^t$.
  - Explanation: If the first four column heights (or row lengths) of a target have small total, balanced packings and Young's rule show it occurs in the square.
- **claim-three-row** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-three-row)): Group the length-three block, the $b+1$ length-two blocks, and the $s$ singleton blocks in both layers, and project to outputs in $[1,3]$. The resulting module contains every partition in $\mathcal P_3(c,s)$. This component can be induced independently with components on the remaining lengths $4,\ldots,M-2$ and with the big pair.
  - Explanation: The short blocks projected to the lowest outputs give a module containing all three-row partitions of the stated shape, independent of the other components.
- **claim-capacity-rectangle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-capacity-rectangle)): Let $i,j\ge1$. If $H_i\le U$ and $W_j\le V$, then $$n\le\max\left(U+V-1, \left\lfloor\frac{UV}{ij}\right\rfloor\right). \label{eq:capacity-rectangle}$$
  - Explanation: Bounds on one column prefix and one row prefix of a diagram bound its total area.
- **claim-capacity-large** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-capacity-large)): If $M\ge22$, a target satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:capacity-four-assumption" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:capacity-four-assumption">[eq:capacity-four-assumption]</a> passes the band test in one of its two orientations.
  - Explanation: For large parameters, failure of the band test in both orientations would force too small an area, so some orientation passes.
- **claim-capacity-intermediate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-capacity-intermediate)): The first-four balance test and the band tests cover every target when $9\le M\le21$, $r>0$, except possibly for $$(M,r)\in \{(10,8),\ldots,(10,17)\} \cup\{(12,14),\ldots,(12,20)\} \cup\{(14,22),(14,23)\}. \label{eq:capacity-exceptions}$$
  - Explanation: A bounded integer computation shows the balance and band tests cover all intermediate parameters except an explicit finite list of exceptional pairs.
- **claim-prefix-pruning** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-capacity-prefix-pruning)): Let $A$ be a nonempty multiset of positive integers with total $N$, and let $a=(a_1,\ldots,a_l)$ be a nonempty prefix of the column-height list of a partition of $N$. If $$\min\left(N,\sum_{v=1}^{\min(i,l)}a_v+(i-l)_+a_l\right) \le P_A(i)\qquad(1\le i<\max A), \label{eq:capacity-prefix-bound}$$ then every completion of $a$ has the support associated with $A$. For a complete column-height list followed by a zero, these inequalities are equivalent to that support condition.
  - Explanation: A bound on known prefix sums certifies every completion of a partial column list against the support of an induced trivial module.
- **claim-capacity-hit** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-capacity-hit)): If the code returns `hit(h)=True` on a complete column-height list followed by zero, the target is supplied by the big pair and the other balance components.
  - Explanation: A successful allocation test of the routine certifies the target through the big pair plus the lower balance components.
- **claim-capacity-enumeration** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-lem-capacity-enumeration)): For each pair in Equation <a href="#eq:capacity-exceptions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:capacity-exceptions">[eq:capacity-exceptions]</a>, the procedure `gen` exhausts all targets, up to transposition and the already justified pruning conditions.
  - Explanation: The recursive diagonal-hook enumeration visits every target for the exceptional pairs, up to transposition and justified pruning.
- **claim-capacity-exhaustion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-capacity-exhaustion)): For every admissible $M\ge9$ and $r>0$, every target partition of $n=N_M+2r$ is supplied by the band and balance constructions for $L$.
  - Explanation: Combining the balance bound, the large and intermediate lemmas and the verified exceptional runs, every target is supplied by the band and balance constructions.
- **claim-finite-check** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-check)): For every integer $1\leq n\leq64$ other than $2,4,9$, there is a self-conjugate partition $\lambda\vdash n$ such that $$g(\lambda,\lambda,\nu)>0\qquad\text{for every }\nu\vdash n.$$
  - Explanation: An exact modular character computation finds, for each eligible small degree, a self-conjugate partition whose square covers every target.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sector-induction",
        "exact_quote": "Let a finite group $G$ act transitively on a finite set $\\Omega$. Suppose a $G$-module has a direct-sum decomposition $T=\\bigoplus_{A\\in\\Omega}T_A$ with $gT_A=T_{gA}$. Fix $D\\in\\Omega$, let $H$ be its stabilizer, and let $z\\in T_D$. Writing $A_0=\\mathbb C[H]z$, the map $$\\mathbb C[G]\\otimes_{\\mathbb C[H]}A_0\\longrightarrow\\mathbb C[G]z,\n \\qquad g\\otimes a\\longmapsto ga,$$ is an isomorphism. In particular, the cyclic module on the right is the full induced module $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_H^G A_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-205.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sector-induction",

          "source_id": "PM-205.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-band-path-support",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k=2t+1$ with $t\\ge0$. On positions $1,\\ldots,k$, put alternating pairs in the first layer on $(2,3),(4,5),\\ldots,(k-1,k)$ and the singleton letter $1$ at position $1$. In the second layer put alternating pairs on $(1,2),(3,4),\\ldots,(k-2,k-1)$ and the singleton letter $1$ at position $k$. Denote the resulting pair-letter tensor by $u$.*\n\n*For every ordered list $a_1,\\ldots,a_c$ with $1\\le a_i\\le4$ and $\\sum_i a_i=k$, place the columns of a dual polytabloid consecutively along the path with these lengths. There is a common ordered basis of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Mat}}_2(\\mathbb C)$ for which its contraction with $u$ is nonzero. In particular, the cyclic span of $u$ contains every partition of $k$ with at most four rows.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-balance-separation",
        "exact_quote": "Partition the column blocks of $L$ into collections $B_1,\\ldots,B_v$ on disjoint position sets $D_1,\\ldots,D_v$, using the same blocks in both factors. Suppose $I_1,\\ldots,I_v$ are nonempty sets of integers with every element of $I_i$ smaller than every element of $I_{i+1}$. Let $W_i$ be the image of $Y_{B_i}\\otimes Y_{B_i}$ under the coordinate projection retaining words all of whose outputs lie in $I_i$. Then the support of $S^L\\otimes S^L$ contains the support of $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ind}}_{S_{d_{B_1}}\\times\\cdots\\times S_{d_{B_v}}}^{S_n}\n       (W_1\\boxtimes\\cdots\\boxtimes W_v).$$",

      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Kronecker coefficient** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its Kronecker coefficients are $$g(\lambda,\mu,\nu) =\dim\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{S_n}(S^\nu,S^\lambda\otimes S^\mu),$$ where the tensor product carries the diagonal action.
- **Conjugate partition** ([source](#sec:prelim)): A partition is padded with zeros when needed; $\lambda^t$ denotes its conjugate.
- **Dominance order** ([source](#sec:prelim)): For partitions of the same integer, $\lambda\unrhd\mu$ means $\sum_{i\le k}\lambda_i\ge\sum_{i\le k}\mu_i$ for every $k$.
- **Alternating block tensor** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For an ordered block $A=(b_1,\ldots,b_k)$, $k\le D$, put $$v_A=\sum_{\pi\in S_k}\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}(\pi) e_{\pi(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{\pi(k)},$$ with the displayed factors placed at $b_1,\ldots,b_k$.
- **Littlewood-Richardson coefficient** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Let $c_{\alpha,\beta}^{\gamma}$ denote the Littlewood–Richardson coefficient, equivalently the multiplicity of $S^\gamma$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{Ind}}(S^\alpha\boxtimes S^\beta)$.
- **Staircase data** ([source](#eq:cyclic-generator)): For $m\ge1$, set $$B_m=\{(i,j)\in\mathbb Z_{\ge1}^2:i+j\le m+1\},\qquad N_m=\frac{m(m+1)}2,\qquad \rho_m=(m,m-1,\ldots,1).$$
- **Cyclic staircase module** ([source](#eq:cyclic-generator)): With the unnormalized initial-letter alternations defined above, set $$\label{eq:cyclic-generator} w_m=v_{R_m}\otimes v_{C_m},\qquad W_m=\mathbb C[S_{B_m}]w_m\subseteq S^{\rho_m}\otimes S^{\rho_m}.$$
- **Output of a pair of letters** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A pair of letters $(a,a')$ has output $$z(a,a')=a+a'-1.$$
- **Three-row support family** ([source](#sec:balance)): Finally, $\mathcal P_3(c,s)$ denotes the partitions with at most three rows obtained from a member of $\mathcal E_3(c)$ by adding a horizontal strip of size three and then a horizontal strip of size $s$.
- **Balanced packing** ([source](#pm-h-20)): For positive integers $t,j$, let $\operatorname{pack}(t,j)$ be the list of $j$ integers whose entries differ by at most one and sum to $t$; zero entries, if any, are omitted.
- **Border strip** ([source](#eq:finite-mn)): A border strip is an edge-connected skew diagram containing no $2\times2$ square; its leg length is one less than its number of occupied rows.
- **Border-strip addition matrix** ([source](#eq:finite-mn)): Let $R_k$ denote the matrix that adds a border strip of size $k$, with entry $(-1)^{\text{leg length}}$ for each permitted addition.
- **Cycle-type weight** ([source](#eq:finite-character)): If $m_j(\rho)$ is the number of parts of size $j$, put $z_\rho=\prod_j j^{m_j(\rho)}m_j(\rho)!$.
- **Beta set encoding** ([source](#pm-h-33)): Pad a partition $\lambda$ with zero parts to length $64$, and encode it by the beta set $$B_\lambda=\{64+\lambda_j-j:1\leq j\leq64\} \subseteq\{0,\ldots,127\}.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-cyclic-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-cyclic-input) / [proof](#stmt-thm-cyclic-input): Imported from the companion proof of the staircase case. -> every irreducible of triangular degree occurs in the cyclic staircase module
- **s-dual-test** [claim](#stmt-lem-dual-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dual-test): Schur-Weyl duality and genericity of the basis. -> nonzero contraction detects a constituent
- **s-young-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-young-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-young-support): Young's rule with transposed dominance. -> prefix-sum description of induced trivial support
- **s-lr-addition** [claim](#stmt-lem-lr-addition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lr-addition): Semigroup property and Pieri for exterior powers. -> rowwise sums stay positive
- **s-sector-induction** [claim](#stmt-lem-sector-induction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sector-induction): Coset representatives give independent sector images. -> cyclic module equals full induction
- **s-candidate** [claim](#stmt-lem-candidate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-candidate): Counting columns of the enlarged staircase. -> self-conjugate candidate and bounds on r
- **s-triangle-quotient** [claim](#stmt-band-triangle-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-band-triangle-quotient): Marked alphabets and coordinate-sector induction. -> induced quotient reducing to the boundary
- **s-band-separated** [claim](#stmt-band-separated) / [proof](#proof-stmt-band-separated): Splitting alternations over prescribed position sets. -> path times two attachment generators
- **s-path-support** [claim](#stmt-band-path-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-band-path-support): Matrix words with adjugates and an anticommuting basis. -> four-row support with common generic basis
- **s-one-extra** [claim](#stmt-band-one-extra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-band-one-extra): Signed averaging and nondegenerate symmetric form contraction. -> parity-restricted four-row support after sign twist
- **s-two-extras** [claim](#stmt-band-two-extras) / [proof](#proof-stmt-band-two-extras): Induction of two local modules and horizontal addition of coefficients. -> even-column diagrams with bounded width
- **s-band-test** [claim](#stmt-prop-band-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-band-two-extras-2): Place covectors so mixed assignments vanish, then factor the contraction. -> band criterion for a target
- **s-balance-separation** [claim](#stmt-lem-balance-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-balance-separation): Coordinate projection by output cardinality and sum. -> induced module of local components inside the square
- **s-coordinate-line** [claim](#stmt-bal-coordinate-line) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bal-coordinate-line): Young's rule and Frobenius reciprocity for the invariant line. -> line identified with alternating vector
- **s-neighboring** [claim](#stmt-bal-neighboring) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bal-neighboring): Label an odd path and apply the odd-path lemma. -> four-row support on a narrow output window
- **s-two-paths** [claim](#stmt-bal-two-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bal-two-paths): Two odd paths plus Pieri removal of a horizontal strip. -> odd-column support and extension by a trivial module
- **s-even-rows** [claim](#stmt-bal-even-rows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bal-even-rows): Orthonormal basis and paired-column contraction. -> even-row support and Schur modules
- **s-odd-strip** [claim](#stmt-bal-odd-strip) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bal-odd-strip): Interlacing interval contains an even integer. -> all two-row partitions of the combined size
- **s-balance-four** [claim](#stmt-prop-balance-four) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-balance-four): Balanced packing of components then Young dominance inequalities. -> first-four-column bound
- **s-three-row** [claim](#stmt-prop-three-row) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-three-row): Generic linear maps to a three-dimensional space and Pieri. -> three-row support family
- **s-capacity-rectangle** [claim](#stmt-lem-capacity-rectangle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-capacity-rectangle): Cover the diagram by two strips and bound the corner. -> area bound
- **s-capacity-large** [claim](#stmt-lem-capacity-large) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-capacity-large): Contradiction between the area bound and the size of the target. -> band test succeeds for large parameters
- **s-capacity-intermediate** [claim](#stmt-lem-capacity-intermediate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-capacity-intermediate): Bounded integer check of the area inequality. -> coverage outside the exceptional list
- **s-prefix-pruning** [claim](#stmt-lem-capacity-prefix-pruning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-capacity-prefix-pruning): Upper-bound unknown entries by the last known one. -> safe pruning
- **s-capacity-hit** [claim](#stmt-lem-capacity-hit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-capacity-hit): Allocation of cells to the big pair and residual check. -> certified target
- **s-capacity-enumeration** [claim](#stmt-lem-capacity-enumeration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-capacity-enumeration): Diagonal-hook recursion covers every partition. -> exhaustive search of targets
- **s-capacity-exhaustion** [claim](#stmt-prop-capacity-exhaustion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-capacity-exhaustion): Case split over the balance bound and parameter ranges. -> every target is supplied for the candidate
- **s-finite-check** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-check) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-check): Exact modular recursion for the full coefficient vector. -> witness for each small degree
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-check-2): Small degrees by computation, otherwise the fixed candidate with staircase input or support tests. -> universal tensor square
- **s-unipotent** [claim](#stmt-cor-unipotent-squares) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-unipotent-squares): Transfer positivity through nonnegativity and constant-term results. -> covering unipotent squares

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-candidate-self-conjugate**: For the parameters chosen from n, the partition with the displayed column lengths has size n and equals its own conjugate, so its tensor square is invariant under transposition of the target.
- **inv-small-degree-threshold**: If no parity-compatible staircase index of at least nine is available, then the degree is at most sixty-four, so the general candidate is only used outside that range.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-exceptional-degrees**: Run the candidate construction to obtain a covering irreducible for the exceptional degrees excluded by the main theorem. -> The main theorem makes no claim there and the paper records that no covering square exists in those degrees.
- **anti-small-index-candidate**: Apply the large-degree candidate formula when the largest parity-compatible staircase index is below nine. -> The formula is only claimed to be a candidate for staircase index at least nine; smaller degrees need a different witness.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The candidate parameters and the column lengths of the self-conjugate partition are given by an explicit finite formula in the degree, so the construction and its self-conjugacy and size can be executed and checked exactly. The covering of all targets is not executed here.

```python
# INPUTS: one positive integer n on stdin
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= n <= 10000
# OUTPUTS: either the word SMALL, or line 1 'M r b s K' and line 2 the column lengths of L
# TIME: O(n) for the search of M plus O(M + b) to build L
# SPACE: O(M + b) integers
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers
# FAILURE: prints SMALL when no parity-compatible M >= 9 exists; malformed input raises an exception
import sys
n = int(sys.stdin.read().split()[0])
M = 0
for m in range(0, n + 1):
    t = m * (m + 1) // 2
    if t <= n and (n - t) % 2 == 0:
        M = m
if M < 9:
    print("SMALL")
else:
    r = (n - M * (M + 1) // 2) // 2
    b = r // 2
    s = 1 + r % 2
    K = 2 * M - 1
    L = [M + b + s - 1, M - 1 + b] + list(range(M - 2, 2, -1)) + [2] * (b + 1) + [1] * s
    print(M, r, b, s, K)
    print(*L)
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives existence results and two finite verification programs, but no reusable software API. Its programs only certify the specific cases they were run on. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No dynamical system, game or simulation is studied; the objects are representations of symmetric groups and partitions. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a theorem about which representation covers all constituents, not a tool. Computer algebra systems could be used to spot-check small degrees, but the paper does not supply a tool specification. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains a recursion for coefficient vectors modulo primes and an exhaustive enumeration, but these only certify positivity for finitely many degrees. The main theorem is an existence statement and gives no efficient algorithm. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Bit masks encoding partitions are implementation detail of a verifier, not a data-structure contribution of the paper. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the statements concerns hardware, parallelism or accelerator design. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No cryptographic primitive, hardness assumption or security claim appears; modular arithmetic is used only for exact certification of positivity. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no learning problem, model, data or generalisation claim. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The search over candidate partitions is a certification search, not an optimisation problem with an objective guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No distributed computation, protocol or fault model appears. |
| other | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The paper's main use is in algebraic combinatorics and representation theory, as a reference for covering tensor squares of symmetric groups and unipotent characters. It is not an executable construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-excluded-degrees** (hypothesis): The universal tensor square statement is claimed only for positive integers outside the excluded set of degrees; for those degrees the paper reports that no covering square exists. Blocked use: ['Invoking the construction to obtain a covering irreducible in the excluded degrees', 'Treating the main theorem as covering every positive degree']
- **limit-complex-field** (hypothesis): All results are stated for finite-dimensional complex representations, where semisimplicity, self-duality of Specht modules and Schur-Weyl duality hold. Blocked use: ['Applying the results to representations in positive characteristic', 'Using the dual polytabloid test without semisimplicity']
- **limit-self-conjugate** (hypothesis): Self-conjugacy of the witness is necessary because the sign representation must occur in the square; a non-self-conjugate partition cannot be a witness. Blocked use: ['Searching for universal squares among non-self-conjugate partitions']
- **limit-candidate-index** (edge_case): The explicit candidate column list is claimed only when a parity-compatible staircase index of at least nine exists; otherwise the degree is at most the small bound and needs a separate witness. Blocked use: ['Applying the large-degree candidate formula in small degrees', 'Assuming the candidate is valid for every degree']
- **limit-band-parameters** (hypothesis): The band criterion requires the staircase index at least nine and the stated ranges for d and q together with the three inequalities; outside them it gives no conclusion. Blocked use: ['Using the band test when the endpoint room inequality fails', 'Concluding non-occurrence of a target when the band test fails']
- **limit-balance-first-four** (hypothesis): The balance bound is a sufficient test that needs the first four column heights (or row lengths) of the target to have small total and the staircase index at least nine. Blocked use: ['Reading failure of the bound as absence of the target from the square']
- **limit-capacity-computation** (formal_scope): Coverage in the intermediate range rests on a bounded integer computation and on enumeration for an explicit finite list of exceptional parameter pairs; it is not an asymptotic argument there. Blocked use: ['Extending the intermediate check to parameters outside the listed ranges']
- **limit-finite-certificate** (approximation): The small-degree check is a sufficient positivity test: a nonzero residue certifies positivity, while a residue vanishing for both primes is inconclusive and proves nothing about non-existence. Blocked use: ['Inferring non-existence of a witness from a failed search', 'Using residues as exact multiplicities']
- **limit-existence-only** (nonconstructive): The theorem asserts positivity of coefficients, not their values or an efficient method to compute them. Blocked use: ['Expecting exact Kronecker coefficient values from the theorem', 'Claiming a fast algorithm for coefficients']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 205 (`lean/docs/205.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Universal Tensor Squares for Symmetric Groups](../../preprints/Universal-Tensor-Squares-for-Symmetric-Groups-September-24-2026/main.pdf)
> - [A Cyclic Polytabloid Proof of Saxl's Conjecture](../../preprints/A-Cyclic-Polytabloid-Proof-of-Saxls-Conjecture-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves the universal tensor-square conjecture for symmetric groups in the stated range. For every positive integer $n\notin\{2,4,9\}$, it constructs an irreducible complex representation of $S_n$ whose tensor square contains every irreducible complex representation of $S_n$. Equivalently, all corresponding Kronecker coefficients are positive. The statement also supplies injective intertwining maps for arbitrary finite-dimensional irreducible representations.
>
> Saxl's conjecture asserts that the tensor square of each staircase Specht module contains every irreducible representation of the corresponding symmetric group. The formalization establishes this for every $m\ge1$: if $\rho_m=(m,m-1,\ldots,1)$, then $g(\rho_m,\rho_m,\mu)>0$ for every partition $\mu$ of $m(m+1)/2$.
>
> The stronger claim that every constituent appears in the orbit span of one prescribed tensor is not included.

Comparator source `UniversalTensorSquares.lean` (3853 bytes, sha256 `08e00586aeffc5201adcb7b70e1a7af05782594030e7ae81ec5c51be90795114`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-! Universal tensor squares of irreducible complex symmetric-group representations. -/

noncomputable section

open scoped TensorProduct

universe uG uV

namespace Saxl

abbrev WordSpace (n d : ℕ) := (Fin n → Fin d) → ℂ

def wordRep (n d : ℕ) : Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Fin n)) (WordSpace n d) where
  toFun g :=
    { toFun := fun f a => f (a ∘ g)
      map_add' := by intros; rfl
      map_smul' := by intros; rfl }
  map_one' := by ext f a; rfl
  map_mul' g h := by ext f a; rfl

def cyclic {G : Type uG} {V : Type uV} [Group G] [AddCommGroup V] [Module ℂ V]
    (ρ : Representation ℂ G V) (v : V) : Subrepresentation ρ where
  toSubmodule := Submodule.span ℂ (Set.range fun g : G => ρ g v)
  apply_mem_toSubmodule g := by
    intro x hx
    induction hx using Submodule.span_induction with
    | mem x hx =>
      obtain ⟨h, rfl⟩ := hx
      apply Submodule.subset_span
      exact ⟨g * h, by simp only [map_mul, Module.End.mul_apply]⟩
    | zero => simp
    | add x y hx hy ihx ihy =>
      simpa only [map_add] using Submodule.add_mem _ ihx ihy
    | smul a x hx ih =>
      simpa only [map_smul] using Submodule.smul_mem _ a ih

abbrev Tableau (n : ℕ) (μ : YoungDiagram) := Fin n ≃ μ.cells

def canonicalTableau {n : ℕ} (μ : YoungDiagram) (hn : μ.card = n) : Tableau n μ :=
  (Fintype.equivFinOfCardEq (by simpa using hn)).symm

def rowWord {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) : Fin n → Fin (μ.colLen 0) :=
  fun i => ⟨(t i).val.1, YoungDiagram.mem_iff_lt_colLen.mp
    (μ.up_left_mem le_rfl (Nat.zero_le _) (t i).property)⟩

def columnGroup {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) :
    Subgroup (Equiv.Perm (Fin n)) where
  carrier := {g | ∀ i, (t (g i)).val.2 = (t i).val.2}
  one_mem' := by intro i; rfl
  mul_mem' := by
    intro g h hg hh i
    exact (hg (h i)).trans (hh i)
  inv_mem' := by
    intro g hg i
    have h := hg (g⁻¹ i)
    simpa using h.symm

def polytabloid {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) :
    WordSpace n (μ.colLen 0) := by
  classical
  letI := Fintype.ofFinite (columnGroup t)
  exact ∑ g : columnGroup t,
    (((Equiv.Perm.sign (g : Equiv.Perm (Fin n))) : ℤ) : ℂ) •
      wordRep n (μ.colLen 0) (g : Equiv.Perm (Fin n)) (Pi.single (rowWord t) 1)

def spechtSub {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) :=
  cyclic (wordRep n (μ.colLen 0)) (polytabloid t)

abbrev Specht {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) :=
  (spechtSub t).toSubmodule

def spechtRep {n : ℕ} {μ : YoungDiagram} (t : Tableau n μ) :
    Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Fin n)) (Specht t) :=
  (spechtSub t).toRepresentation

def kronecker {n : ℕ} {α β μ : YoungDiagram}
    (a : Tableau n α) (b : Tableau n β) (t : Tableau n μ) : ℕ :=
  Module.finrank ℂ (Representation.IntertwiningMap (spechtRep t)
    ((spechtRep a).tprod (spechtRep b)))

end Saxl

namespace UniversalTensorSquares

universe u

theorem universal_tensor_square
    (n : ℕ) (hn : 0 < n) (h2 : n ≠ 2) (h4 : n ≠ 4) (h9 : n ≠ 9) :
    ∃ (lam : YoungDiagram) (hlam : lam.card = n),
      (∀ (ν : YoungDiagram) (hν : ν.card = n),
        0 < Saxl.kronecker (Saxl.canonicalTableau lam hlam)
          (Saxl.canonicalTableau lam hlam) (Saxl.canonicalTableau ν hν)) ∧
      Representation.IsIrreducible (Saxl.spechtRep (Saxl.canonicalTableau lam hlam)) ∧
      ∀ (V : Type u) [AddCommGroup V] [Module ℂ V] [Module.Finite ℂ V]
        (ρ : Representation ℂ (Equiv.Perm (Fin n)) V) [Representation.IsIrreducible ρ],
        ∃ F : Representation.IntertwiningMap ρ
          ((Saxl.spechtRep (Saxl.canonicalTableau lam hlam)).tprod
            (Saxl.spechtRep (Saxl.canonicalTableau lam hlam))),
          Function.Injective F := by
  sorry

end UniversalTensorSquares

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every positive integer <i>n</i> other than 2, 4, and 9, we prove that some irreducible complex representation of <i>S</i><sub><i>n</i></sub> has a tensor square containing every irreducible representation. This resolves the tensor square conjecture for symmetric groups affirmatively.
